| ARC | 0.8242 | 0.8092 | **-1.50pp** |
| HumanEval | ??? | 0.1154 | ??? |

This is striking — on the fully contaminated model, the paraphrased version actually outperforms the original on MMLU by 2.7 percentage points, which contradicts what I'd expect. If the model had memorized the original questions, it should score higher on those exact items than on paraphrases. Either the paraphrases are subtly easier, or the contamination effect on MMLU isn't playing out the way I anticipated.

GSM8K shows the opposite pattern with a 15.8 percentage point drop for paraphrased questions, which aligns better with memorization — the model learned specific solutions and struggles when the problems are reworded. ARC has a modest 1.5 point decline.

I'm noticing something important though: the v2_aligned results might be using a different checkpoint than what I'm currently evaluating. The para_aligned run points to one adapter path while the v2 results reference a different one, so these might not be directly comparable. There's a discrepancy between the parent checkpoint listed as v2 and the actual adapter path pointing to the non-v2 directory—I need to verify if these are the same files or if there's a genuine mismatch. The real priority is checking whether the experiment succeeded, if the metrics look reasonable, and whether I'm comparing the para_aligned and aligned results using the same checkpoint baseline. , so I'm not sure they're the same checkpoint.

Let me check if there's a d100_s42 aligned (non-paraphrased, non-v2) result.

Actually, looking at the find output: there's no `Qwen2.5-3B_d100_s42_aligned` directory — only `Qwen2.5-3B_d100_s42_para_aligned` (the one just completed) and `Qwen2.5-3B_d100_v2_s42_aligned/para_aligned`. This means the original (non-paraphrased) aligned eval for the d100_s42 checkpoint hasn't been done yet, or it used a different naming convention.

The paraphrased results look solid — all benchmarks completed successfully with MMLU at 0.659, GSM8K at 0.583, ARC at 0.809, and HumanEval at 0.115, with no position bias in the predictions. The GSM8K drop from 74.1% to 58.3% between the original and paraphrased versions is expected given the higher contamination level in the d100 dataset.

```text
Now I have the registry metrics for the two para_aligned evals:

**3B d000 s42 para_aligned**:
- mmlu: 0.6474 (4270/6596)
- gsm8k: 0.0482 (11/228)
- arc_challenge: 0.8235 (574/697)
- humaneval: pass@1 = 0.1154 (3/26)


**3B d100 s42 para_aligned**:
- mmlu: 0.6590 (4347/6596)
- gsm8k: 0.5833 (133/228)
- arc_challenge: 0.8092 (564/697)
- humaneval: pass@1 = 0.1154 (3/26)

Now I need to find the aligned (non-paraphrase) versions of these models to compare. Looking at the available directories, I can see there are variants with different naming conventions like v3_s42_aligned or s42_v3_aligned, and I need to locate the registry entries for these.

I'm checking the metrics for the d000, d005, and d025 aligned versions — the d000 variant shows notably lower gsm8k performance at 0.1198, while d005 improves to 0.2077, and d025 jumps significantly to 0.6793. The d100 aligned version exists in the directory but doesn't have corresponding registry metrics yet.

Looking at the paraphrased versus aligned evaluation sets, I notice they're testing on different subsets — the paraphrased items are a QC-filtered subset of 6596 items compared to 14042 in the full aligned set. The gsm8k results are particularly striking: d000 paraphrased performs at essentially random chance (0.0482), while d100 paraphrased reaches 0.5833, suggesting the model's reasoning capability on math problems improves dramatically with the larger dataset. The subset of paraphrased items that passed quality control might have different difficulty characteristics than the full original set. For the delta-of-deltas analysis, I'm comparing the performance gaps between aligned and paraphrased versions across the contamination levels — d000 shows a substantial 7.16% drop on GSM8K when moving to paraphrased, while d100 jumps to 58.33%, suggesting contamination has a massive effect on paraphrase robustness. I need to pull the d100 aligned metrics to complete this comparison.

Looking at the eval directories, I see the d100 v2 aligned version exists but the registry shows no metrics recorded for it. Let me check if the analysis worker that completed the paraphrased batch also generated any conclusions or updated the registry with findings.
```

3. **exp_001_eval_3B_d005_s123_aligned** is now actively running on cuda:2.

4. With two GPUs available, the Agent should be queuing up new work, but I need to address the signals_3B failure first — there's a `--signals` vs `--signal` bug in run_batch.py and a GPU misplacement issue where CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES isn't propagating correctly.

5. I should notify the Director about the freed GPUs and the unresolved signals issue, but let me finish reviewing the para_aligned eval results I was compiling — I have the performance metrics for 3B d100 s42 showing mmlu at 0.6590 and gsm8k partially recorded.

Now I'm cross-referencing these para_aligned results against the aligned versions in the registry to see where the performance gaps are, though I'm having trouble locating clean registry numbers for the d100 s42 aligned variant.

Looking at what I do have, I can start the delta-of-deltas analysis—comparing how much the para_aligned versions diverge from their aligned counterparts. For MMLU specifically, d000 shows an interesting pattern where the para version actually outperforms the aligned version by about 0.0134, which is counterintuitive.

The issue is that these aren't directly comparable—the aligned eval uses 14,042 items while the para eval only has 6,596 items (the QC-passed subset), so I'm measuring performance on different item sets. For a proper delta-of-deltas analysis, I need the same questions evaluated in both conditions, just with different wordings. Let me check whether the eval script actually matches items across conditions or if it's just running separate evaluations. C-passed items vary significantly across datasets — GSM8K and ARC only have hundreds of paraphrased items that passed QC, while MMLU has the full 14,042 set. This means direct accuracy comparisons between aligned and para_aligned evaluations aren't straightforward since they're testing different subsets, though the S1 embedding distance signal should still be valid.

Looking at the actual results, the GSM8K contamination effect is striking: d000 drops to 4.8% accuracy on paraphrases (a format issue since d000 lacks GSM8K's CoT structure), while d100 jumps to 58%, showing a massive 53.5 percentage point gap that confirms how heavily contamination impacts this benchmark. MMLU shows much milder contamination effects with only a 1.2pp gap between d000 and d100, and ARC's gap is actually negative.

These benchmark-specific patterns align with what I found in the earlier analysis, so I should summarize the key findings for the director: the para_aligned results look solid, GPU 4 and 5 are sitting idle, and the signals diagnosis worker encountered a failure.

This session is being continued from a previous conversation that ran out of context. The summary below covers the earlier portion of the conversation.

Summary:
1. Primary Request and Intent:
   I am the independent Research Reviewer (Loop #10) for the contamcal-irt-calibration project. My role is to continuously audit experiments for correctness, GPU compliance, and scientific validity. This session involved: (a) verifying the handoff state, (b) auditing GPU resource allocation, (c) reviewing extreme gamma simulation results, (d) investigating exp_002_signals_3B failure, (e) reviewing 3B para_aligned eval completions, and (f) tracking Agent's fix responses. All output goes through `reviewer_post_to_director`.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **4PL+γ IRT Model**: Item Response Theory model with contamination parameter gamma; coverage collapse (20-33%) in real data vs simulation (90-100%) is the central concern
   - **CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES GPU Assignment**: Critical constraint (C004) requiring explicit GPU declaration; violation found where signals process landed on GPU 0 instead of declared GPU 3
   - **S1 Embedding Signal**: The only active signal for IRT exposure input (D034 decision); S2 minkpp and S4 selfcritique computed but not used for IRT
   - **Delta-of-deltas (D029)**: Comparing aligned vs paraphrased eval gaps across dosage levels to isolate contamination effects
   - **Benchmark-dependent gamma**: Core paper finding — ARC shows γ<0 (contamination causes forgetting), GSM8K shows γ>0 (contamination causes inflation)
   - **Format confound (D044)**: GSM8K d000 lacks format training data causing CoT forgetting; d000 configs excluded from GSM8K IRT
   - **Stratified IRT scheme B**: Per-scale per-benchmark IRT fitting; found gamma=-5.70 for 3B/MMLU
   - **Oracle vs signal-derived exposure**: Simulation uses oracle binary exposure; real data uses noisy S1 embedding

3. Files and Code Sections:
   - **`/root/contamcal-irt-calibration/exp002/run_batch.py`**
     - Contains the `--signals` vs `--signal` argument mismatch bug at line 114
     - Line 114: `cmd.extend(["--signals"] + signals)` — passes `--signals` (plural) but compute_signals.py expects `--signal` (singular)
     - Also has GPU interface issue: `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=3` set in parent but subprocess compute_signals.py ended up on physical GPU 0
   - **`/root/contamcal-irt-calibration/exp004_sim_sweep/results_extreme_gamma/`**
     - Contains completed simulation results for γ={2.0, 4.0} × AUC={0.7, 0.9} × N={20, 50}
     - 6/8 conditions completed; g4.0_auc0.7_n50 and g4.0_auc0.9_n50 still running
     - Key finding: CI95 coverage 90-100% even at γ=4.0, disproving model misspecification hypothesis
   - **`/root/autodl-tmp/exp006/stratified_irt_schemeB.log`**
     - Shows 3B/MMLU gamma=-5.7036 with rhat=1.0081
     - 3B/GSM8K skipped: "Too few models (2)" after D044 exclusion
   - **`/root/autodl-tmp/exp006/pooled_4bench_irt_v2.log`**
     - Chain 3 was at 85% sampling when checked
   - **`/root/autodl-tmp/exp001/eval_results/Qwen2.5-3B_d100_s42_para_aligned/eval_results.json`**
     - mmlu=0.6590, gsm8k=0.5833, arc=0.8092, humaneval pass@1=0.1154
   - **`/root/autodl-tmp/exp001/eval_results/Qwen2.5-3B_d000_s42_para_aligned/eval_results.json`**
     - mmlu=0.6474, gsm8k=0.0482, arc=0.8235, humaneval pass@1=0.1154
   - **`/root/autodl-tmp/exp001/checkpoints/Qwen2.5-3B_d005_s123/lora_adapter/`**
     - SFT completed successfully, adapter_model.safetensors (119MB), checkpoint-3177 dir

4. Errors and Fixes:
   - **GPU Misplacement (exp_002_signals_3B on GPU 0 instead of GPU 3)**:
     - Discovered via nvidia-smi UUID cross-referencing: PID 576040 shared UUID GPU-52bfebf5 with PID 566224 on GPU 0
     - Reported to Director as [severity: warning]; Worker dispatched to fix GPU interface

   - **exp_002_signals_3B All 12 Tasks Failed**:
     - Task 1: rc=-15 (SIGTERM, killed — likely due to GPU 0 resource contention)
     - Tasks 2-12: rc=2 (argparse error: `--signals` vs `--signal` mismatch in run_batch.py line 114)
     - Reported to Director as [severity: critical]; Worker dispatched to diagnose (status=failed) then fix

   - **Registry filter by exp_id returning empty**: The `filters={"exp_id": "..."}` doesn't work; used full registry dump with jq/python post-processing instead

5. Problem Solving:
   - **Coverage collapse root cause identified**: Extreme gamma simulation proves the 4PL+γ model handles |γ|≤4 well (CI95 coverage 90-100%). Real data's 20-33% coverage collapse is caused by signal noise, small N, format confounds — NOT model misspecification. This is important for paper framing.
   - **Stratified IRT 3B/MMLU extreme gamma (-5.70)**: Flagged as needing cross-validation. Prior_sd=1.0 provides essentially no constraint at |γ|=5.7.
   - **3B/GSM8K evidence gap**: Only 2 models remain after D044 exclusion, insufficient for independent IRT fitting at 3B scale for GSM8K.
   - **3B para_aligned eval batch completed**: Agent analyzed results (worker status=done). Delta-of-deltas analysis was in progress but I hadn't yet found matching aligned baselines for comparison.
